ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u90	Thank you, President. Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen. The pandemic has brought us many changes in our daily lives: changes in the education of our children, changes in the perception of our priorities, changes in our work model and in the new way of developing work. We have seen how governments, in order to protect people's lives and health, were forced to stop economic activity, and this, of course, has had a particularly serious impact on employment. Our Government, unlike in other crises, has prioritized the protection of individuals and workers, and it has been protected by putting in place all the mechanisms within its reach: ERTE, which has avoided the destruction of thousands of jobs; unprecedented aid to the self-employed; the implementation of the minimum living income to protect the most vulnerable; and the Royal Decree-Law expedites the procedures for the recognition of this non-contributory benefit, as well as the retroactive nature of the applications; the extension of the MeCaida Plan, and, as something new also, the regulation of the provision of the employment relationship at a distance, that is, outside the establishments and the usual centres of the enterprise, using new technologies. Coronavirus containment has meant for many workers the first teleworking experience in Spain and has spread to 30%, being its use before the residual pandemic and lacking specific regulation. These workers must be thanked for having put their personal means in place so that the companies would not stop. It is to be thanked - and this is not to the gentlemen of VOX - that it was those workers who initially put all the means at their disposal to keep working. That is why this Government is taking the necessary measures to regulate teleworking, a regulation that is the result of social dialogue and from the framework of labour relations already initiated in the European Union. This is a necessary and basic rule insofar as it envisages the essential elements to ensure equality in the provision of work within and outside the workplace, leaving to collective bargaining its role of balancing the employment relationship. Honourable Members of the PP, Article 13 had become obsolete. New technologies allow for a new organization of work. This rule has taken the measures that have been pursued, on the one hand, to care for workers and, on the other, to facilitate the continuity of business activity or their immediate reactivation after the state of alarm. To this end, article 5 of Royal Decree-Law 5/2020, which established the preferential nature of distance work, has already been adopted and has been shown to be a good tool to contain mobility, restrict contacts and combat the pandemic and also to increase efficiency in our work. We need to be aware that telework has come to stay and can have a positive impact on the economy: it facilitates accessibility to work, allows time flexibility, allows the reconciliation of family and work life, helps the motivation of workers and increases productivity, reduces labour costs, avoids displacements and - I would like to highlight it among other things - means that it can be considered one of the main drivers of economic development in rural areas. I hope and hope that teleworking will be that opportunity that we expect in small peoples for our economic development and thus for the people to settle in the territory. To that end, Minister, we must ensure a good digital connection. It is fundamental; if not, there will continue to be inequality, and I firmly believe in this Government's commitment not to leave anyone behind, and the men and women we live in the people neither. In particular, today we cannot leave rural women behind. Today, International Rural Women's Day, we must continue to develop policies of equality in the rural world, policies that in some communities, such as Andalusia, are suffering a serious and important cut, which we cannot consent to. [[applause]] This royal decree-law is the result of social dialogue and places us within the European framework of distance work, as we have said. It was a necessary and profound challenge, which takes place in three respects: firstly, these workers will benefit from the same rights as those recognized in the legislation and collective agreements as those working on the premises of companies, and that is why there are basic aspects, such as the provision of means, equipment, tools and consumables, the payment and compensation of expenses, the right to training and professional promotion, the right to the prevention of occupational risks, privacy and data protection; secondly, - as has already been said - the voluntary and reversible nature of the worker and of the employer is recognized, and it must be reflected in a written agreement - Mr de VOX, agreements are not impossible, an agreement can be reached - and, thirdly, this form of work must be provided with greater protection, ensuring flexibility in its use. These new forms of employment also present new challenges and require the development of a labour regulatory framework, always in consultation with the social partners - as has already been said here - which combines the recognition of the new rights with legal certainty in the provision of the employment relationship for both workers and employers. Among the challenges of this normative development we must take into account the guarantee of preserving occupational health as a basic right of the worker, guaranteeing the right to disconnection - this is fundamental - and the gender impact, an issue that must be addressed in order to prevent this form of employment provision from becoming something common to women, who are the most demanding workers in companies. Although, as we have said, telework facilitates reconciliation, it risks women being the most supportive, which in many cases would entail taking on more family responsibilities, thus perpetuating the traditional gender role. We have seen and suffered during the confinement, ladies and gentlemen. When schools were closed, telework penalized women twice, because we are still the ones who take care of the home. That is why it is so necessary to move forward and regulate it, and, if it is the result of consensus, better. We would like to thank the Ministry and the Minister for their ability to reach agreements with trade unions and employers. And they could already learn some political parties, instead of being in perpetual confrontation. Ladies and gentlemen, let us build the future with the help of technology, but in conditions of justice, defending decent and quality employment, defending the rights of workers, as the Socialist Party has always done for one hundred and forty-one years, and creating new rights for the future. The meaning of our vote will be in favour. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u93	Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, very good afternoon to all of you. Greetings. I come to this plenary session of the Congress to ask for the vote in favor of validation... [[Rumors]]
ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u94	Wait a minute, Minister. If you leave the Chamber, do it quietly, please. Go ahead, minister.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u95	Thank you very much, President. He said that he was coming to this plenary of Congress to ask his colleagues to vote in favour of the validation of Royal Decree-Law 29/2020 of 29 September of urgent measures in the field of teleworking in public administrations and human resources in the National Health System to deal with the health crisis caused by the COVID-19, which we adopted in the Council of Ministers on 29 September. I will therefore divide my intervention into two parts: the first will be on teleworking in public administrations and the second on human resources in the National Health System. I therefore begin with teleworking and I would like to share with your ladies and gentlemen the conditions under which this royal decree has been drawn up. I begin with my thanks to all the people and institutions who have been part of this great work that the technical teams of the different public administrations and also of the trade union organizations have done, as well as to each of those responsible for the will to communicate and to understand that they have demonstrated. I would also like to express - and I think it is shared - the recognition of the work of all public employees, who have made it possible for the provision of public services to be a reality, despite the difficulties we have often had to overcome. [[Applauds]] Thank you, ladies and gentlemen. My appreciation to all of them and all of them. They have heard me say many times from this rostrum and in the public interventions that I have had occasion to realize that it is absolutely essential that we be able to build among all and achieve a common minimum of understanding, a common minimum of understanding that this country needs today more than ever, ladies and gentlemen, to continue to move forward and progress. Well, this royal decree-law, on the teleworking side, is a good example of what I think it means to do politics in a good way, to do good politics. I believe that we all agreed that the need to regulate this organizational modality was imperative, especially in the times we live in, because so far, in some way, the reality is that we had advanced to our own legal consideration or regulation, even though we had some agreements at international level, other agreements at European level, some rule, but none with the cancellation and incardination that this one must have in the Basic Staff Regulations. It is true that before the pandemic, during the pandemic and at the end the ministry I have the honour to preside over has issued resolutions that have allowed us to spend all this time and that the regulation of distance work in public administrations has been possible, but it was absolutely imperative that it could be regulated and reflected in the Basic Staff Regulations and therefore applicable to all public administrations. This regulation, ladies and gentlemen, is the result of consensus, the result of dialogue and intense work between the autonomous communities, the Spanish Federation of Municipalities and Provinces and the Government of Spain, and also the result of a great deal of agreement at the general bureau of the trade unions that make up it, the General Bureau of Negotiation. All this process to regulate teleworking in public administrations begins on 11 June, when a conference of public administrations takes place after bilateral meetings that had been held by those who spoke to each and every one of the leaders of the autonomous public administrations of this country. At that conference we agreed on who we spoke, that is, the Government of Spain, the autonomous communities and the Spanish Federation of Municipalities, to regulate teleworking, and, moreover, we agreed to do so jointly. To this end, ladies and gentlemen, within the Committee on the Coordination of Public Employment, we set up a working group, so that this group, which has met around three times before the final approval, was the one to draft together - I mean, the drafting board of autonomous communities, the Spanish Federation of Municipalities and the Government of Spain - a modification of this kind, that is to say, from above in a participatory and shared way. Therefore, I also thank those who have made this wording possible. I reiterate, ladies and gentlemen, that it is the result of agreement and dialogue, from below, because I believe that it gives it important legitimacy, not only in law, but also in fact in the terms expressed by Professor Jürgen Habermas. I think it is very important: legal legitimacy and also de facto legitimacy. That is why I have started my speech by thanking all the parties, in particular the trade unions that have signed this agreement at the general table, that is, Commissions, CSIF and UGT, to whom I would like to acknowledge their continued willingness to dialogue and to reach agreements. And also, ladies and gentlemen, you will allow me to thank my colleague, the Minister of Finance, and her entire team - our collaboration is close, but in this particular regulation - and therefore make it public from this rostrum. Some considerations, as a flash, if I may, of what telework in public administrations means. First of all, it is a pioneering rule, ladies and gentlemen, which introduces a comprehensive regulation of a new mode of service provision, which is teleworking. This royal decree-law implies an amendment of the EBEP, adding a new article, 47 bis, which will apply, given its basic character, to two and a half million employees and public employees throughout the country, therefore in all the public administrations of this country. The purpose of emergency legislation is defined by the jurisprudence of the Constitutional Court and by the pandemic situation in which we find ourselves and also by regulating, as we have done, telework in the private sector. We felt that it was very important that the public and private sectors should also be at hand in this regulation. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, basic rule, rule applicable to all public administrations, rule applicable to both civil servants and workers and, therefore, a common framework shared between all public administrations. Each public administration will have a period of six months, if it so wishes, to adapt this regulation of EBEP to its own respective autonomous or territorial scope. Characteristics and singularities that I'm going to comment on in order to request your validation. First, this regulation is a new form of organization of work in public administrations. Secondly, this mode of delivery of work can be developed as long as the needs of the service permit and, therefore, it is obviously done outside the units of the Administration through the use of information and communication technologies. Moreover, this mode of work is neither ordinary nor absolute. The ordinary mode remains the face-to-face mode and, in addition, each Administration will determine how face-to-face can be combined with telework, always guaranteeing, ladies and gentlemen, direct or face-to-face attention to citizens in the provision of public services. In addition, teleworking is defined in this regulation on a voluntary and reversible basis. Therefore, voluntarity and reversibility are two key vectors of this regulation that we have agreed and that we present to you today. Of course, tasks that are likely to be teleworking must be able to be teleworking and, moreover, obviously, the public employee must have the digital skills necessary for his performance. Moreover, ladies and gentlemen, of course, any public employee, whatever form of work he performs, has the same rights and duties in any of them. In addition, ladies and gentlemen, public administrations must equip public employees with the appropriate technological means to carry out this mode. However, each public administration, since we are talking about a basic regulation, that is, a regulation of minimums so that each autonomous or municipal administration can develop it, even the General Administration of the State, it will be the one that has to define the technological means for this performance. In addition, ladies and gentlemen, this modality must be expressly authorized by the administrative unit of the administration concerned and must also be carried out in accordance with objective criteria for access and, as I have said, compatible with the face-to-face modality, and in no case, of course, can result in non-compliance with the working day or the working hours of the same. Ladies and gentlemen, without prejudice to the voluntary nature of this service, you must, of course, ensure that the needs of the service are met at every moment and in every place. I would like to share with all of you that the value of this measure means opening a path for this government of progress towards a 21st century administration, a 21st century public administration that, as President Sánchez has already announced in the presentation of the space of the Plan Spain can, is part of one of the ten lever policies. Ladies and gentlemen, for the first time the public administration is going to be part of one of the lever policies that will allow our country to address this plan of reforms, investments, transformations and resilience in order, without a doubt, to reach a point of inflection, a point of inflection in this aspect that I am commenting on, which we also hope and wish - and that is the determination of this government to which I am a party - to be a point of non-return, to recover and properly weigh the value of the public. Always but especially in these circumstances of difficulty we have found the value of the public. Without the public, without public institutions and without public employees, the response to this pandemic would not have been the same. That is why I am grateful for the recognition that this House has made when I spoke about public employees. And, moreover, to contribute, ladies and gentlemen, to the great transformations that we have ahead in this country, where telework is going to be a fundamental milestone, and it is already in the National Plan for Recovery, Transformation and Resilience. Having said that, I am going to comment on the second part or the other major issue that regulates this royal decree-law, which are the health measures that the Ministry of Health has incorporated into this royal decree-law because of urgent need. I would like to point out to you, ladies and gentlemen, that these are absolutely exceptional measures, the sole objective of which is to strengthen the National Health System throughout the national territory. Therefore, exceptional and transitional measures, as established by the Royal Decree-Law itself, in the field of human resources of our National Health System that reproduce - listen well - the measures that have already been taken during the state of alarm. Therefore, these are not new measures, they are measures that have already taken place in Spain as a result of the state of alarm throughout the country and that make it possible - they do not mandate - for the autonomous communities, if they wish, to hire health professionals in the face of the overload of the system and the pressure of assistance. I want to mention and share - and I do, if I may, in a preventive way, understand correctly - that this measure has been included at the request of the Autonomous Communities. It is a request that this Government of Spain understands well and stands alongside the Autonomous Communities and that has been made absolutely urgently, because urgent was the situation of many Autonomous Communities, and especially that of one of them, which has requested it. I would therefore like to share with you, ladies and gentlemen, that one day before the adoption of this royal decree-law, when it was included, an extraordinary meeting of the Human Resources Commission of the National Health System was held to address this situation and propose measures, and so it was. I would also like to say that there is documentary record of what I am saying to you, so that no one has any doubt of the intention, of the good intention of this Government and of the Minister, my colleague Illa, of why we have introduced into this royal decree these measures on human resources of the National Health System: because it has been at the express request of the Autonomous Communities, and this Government, understanding that there could be other ways, has understood the exceptionality and urgency of the Autonomous Communities, and, as such, we have expressed and collected it; at the request of the Autonomous Communities, ladies and gentlemen, with documentary record, and, if they do not have it, here I have the documentation that supports it. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, we give an urgent and immediate response. We were asked at the meeting of the COVID group and we were asked in writing, because there was a lack of health personnel and we understood the reasons for urgency and peremptivity that the Autonomous Communities were sending us. We responded and allowed the autonomous communities or Ingesa to hire those health professionals who, having conducted selective tests of specialized health training, would not have obtained a place in the 2019-2020 call of the MIR. In this regard, ladies and gentlemen, the Ministry of Health has already transferred to the Autonomous Communities, which are competent in the field of health, the list of the 13,880 health and non-health professionals who may be available during this transitional period and as an exceptional measure the Autonomous Communities have it for their benefit. On the other hand, ladies and gentlemen, it is possible for specialists who are not Community doctors - therefore outside the scope of the European Union and provided that the evaluation committee has issued the proposal report, i.e. it is in the first stage of the processing dossier - to be recruited if the reasons are urgent and the autonomous community so decides. Also, ladies and gentlemen, this royal decree includes the possibility that the autonomous communities, which are the ones with competence in the field of health, can have both medical and nursing personnel to care for the units in the hospitals or other different areas and primary care if there were deficits. Ladies and gentlemen, we are aware that this measure, just as it has its pros, has its cons, but in any case I would like to reiterate that it has been in response to the request made and given the evolution of the pandemic itself and the situation of resurgence in this second wave. Many autonomous communities have expressed this, and that is what this Government has intended by coming to the call of the autonomous communities, because we believe deeply in co-governance, because we believe in co-responsibility and because we want to be at the side of the autonomous communities in the performance of their competences in the best possible way. We have done so with the COVID Fund: EUR 16 billion for the first time, unprecedented, non-reimbursable, for the Autonomous Communities; EUR 9 billion only for health expenditure, understanding that the Autonomous Communities have borne the brunt of this expenditure. The Spanish Government has therefore assumed the deficit as a result of this extra amount added to the Autonomous Communities: 9000 for health expenditure, 5000 for expenditure in the case of loss of income and 2000 for educational expenditure. Ladies and gentlemen, we believe that these are important measures to help, I insist, the Autonomous Communities, together with a procedure which was also essential in order to leave no doubt, to amend the Law on the Management of Health Professions so that some tests can be carried out in a telematic way, by electronic means, which also greatly facilitates their scope of action. For my part, ladies and gentlemen, nothing more. I'll be at your disposal. I thank you for your attention and ask for a position in favour of the adoption of this rule to the extent that you consider it appropriate and desirable. Thank you very much, Your Honor. ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u100	Thank you very much, Mr. Guitarte. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Thank you very much, Mrs. Guille. For the Basque Parliamentary Group, I have the floor Mr. Legarda Uriarte. [[EAJ-PNV]]
ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u101	Thank you very much, President. We are discussing the validation of Royal Decree-Law 29/2020, which, first of all, regulates teleworking in the field of public administrations, of staff at their service, both officials and labour personnel, once the latter staff, labour, was excluded from the scope of Royal Decree-Law 28/2020. This decree-law, which we are now discussing, introduces in its first article a new article 47 bis in the Basic Statute of the Public Employee, which, in our view, faithfully respects the previous agreements reached with the Autonomous Communities, without prejudice to introducing a novel aspect after its negotiation with the trade union representation as to whether the Administration should provide and maintain the technological means necessary for persons working in this mode; an obligation on the other hand, on which we have no objection, since it seems reasonable from any point of view. As for the content of the new precept of EBEP, from our point of view too, it limits its scope correctly to what should be a regulation of the basic notes of this form of telework, such as the same concept of telework, its suitability with the content of the job, the principle of necessity, its compatibility with face-to-face work, its voluntariness and reversibility, except exceptional assumptions, and the equality of rights and duties with regard to face-to-face work. Apart from these nuclear aspects, which are common to any Administration, the specific provision of telework must, as already indicated, be adapted to the conditions and characteristics of each Administration, after consultation with the representation of the workers, as also indicated in the second paragraph of this Article 47a, when it provides that the provision of the service shall be carried out in the terms of the rules which are laid down in the development of this basic statute, which shall be the subject of collective negotiation in the relevant field and shall include objective criteria in the access to this mode of provision of services, in full compliance with our view, also with the scope and concept of what should be a basic regulation and in particular what should be achieved in the fulfilment of the bases of the statutory legal regime for staff serving public administrations and which should allow a reasonable margin of development for each Administration to develop and implement it, in the sense precisely established by the High Court with regard to what should consist of this State competence under Article 149(118a) of the Constitution. In short, by establishing the common legal denominator necessary for application throughout the territory from which each autonomous community can, in defence of its own interests, introduce the peculiarities which it considers appropriate within the framework of competence assigned to it by its statute in the relevant field. In turn, on the other hand - it has also been pointed out, ladies and gentlemen - the decree-law that we discussed, on 29/2020, contains a second part with regard to other regulations relating to the human resources of the health system for an initial period of twelve months, which can be extended for periods of three months and according to the needs of the health crisis arising from the COVID-19. Thus, the Autonomous Communities and also the National Institute of Health Management are allowed new types and modalities of recruitment so that, if they so require, they have a greater number of professionals to relieve the pressure to which the health system may be subjected and, as stated in the explanatory statement, to ensure an adequate service to the citizens to whom they serve. In addition, these same managers of the National Health System, also on a transitional and exceptional basis, are allowed to assign medical and nursing personnel to jobs and care devices other than their own in the same or other workplace, and also to the same end to have a greater number of health professionals where they are most needed. In short, ladies and gentlemen, these are measures to deal with the lack of health professionals at the present time; measures that were already in place, as has been said, during the state of alarm but which fell at its end; measures that, at the time, helped, but let us not forget the fear that they also raised among health system professionals by enabling, if not acting with precise caution, a negative impact on public health, either by placing contracted personnel without the qualification of a specialist in tasks that exceeded their training or by transferring health personnel from the National Health System, on this occasion, to posts whose content was not consistent with their knowledge or experience. As we have all had the opportunity to read in the press or to listen in the media, these fears have reappeared by taking a new course in these measures with this Decree-Law 29/2020, measures which we believe should have been the subject of further debate and deliberation with health system professionals in order to, precisely, annoy their fears. We also believe that they should have been the subject of further discussion and deliberation with the Autonomous Communities, as the specific needs were brought to their attention just the day before the Council of Ministers approved them. As the minister has told us, it is true that it was at the request of some autonomous communities who needed it or believe they need it, but it cannot be ignored that it would have been appropriate to analyse the effects of these measures while they were in force and whether they had caused some distortion to justify the fears that were expressed at the time and that have also been expressed today. These fears - we must also point out - coincide in the present time with the movement and discomfort of the MIR collective regarding the bad contractual and labor conditions in which they consider that they are subjected in their labor and training activity for the acquisition of their specialized health certification. I'm finishing up. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, we believe that these are measures which, in order not to have a negative impact on public health, must be used in an undiminished manner and with due caution, including the appropriate and necessary collective bargaining, in particular in the case of secondments of health workers to jobs other than their own. Ladies and gentlemen, without prejudice to the cautions noted, we shall vote in favour of the content of the decree-law discussed by voting in favour of its validation. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u130	Thank you, Minister. - REAL DECRETO-LEY 30/2020, OF 29 SEPTEMBER, OF SOCIAL MEASURES IN DEFENCE OF EMPLOYMENT. [[File number 130/000031]]
ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u131	Validation or repeal of Royal Decree-Law 30/2020 of 29 September of social measures in defence of employment. To present the royal decree-law, the Minister of Labour and Social Economy has the floor on behalf of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u132	Good afternoon again. I rise again in this rostrum to request from its lords the validation of Royal Decree-Law 30/2020, which is the result of the III Agreement in defense of employment between social agents and the Government. It is no longer an agreement despite being already the sixth reached by this Ministry of Labour in the framework of social dialogue. The third agreement in defence of employment is the result of many days of joint reflection, of shared perceptions - also of dissensions - and it is above all, as I have pointed out earlier, the emblem of a climate of dialogue and cooperation between the Government and the social actors, which shows us a path contrary to that of the rift and which lies beyond noise and no for the no. Indeed, ladies and gentlemen, agreements such as the one that sustains this royal decree-law not only strengthen us as a country, but bring certainty in a difficult and constantly changing context such as that in which the health, social and economic crisis caused by the pandemic has placed us. The third social agreement in defence of employment, which is the basis, soul and subject matter of this royal decree-law that we bring to your consideration today, was reached after many days of joint reflection on a fortunately contrasted evidence that the temporary employment regulation dossiers, the so-called ERTE, have fulfilled their objective in substance and in form. They have stopped the fall in employment, saved many of our small, medium and large businesses, our self-employed and protected the most vulnerable. The fundamental pillar of this rule is once again the internal flexibility measures in companies, which aim to stabilise employment, as well as to prevent the destruction of jobs and the productive capacity of the business fabric in the face of a situation, this time, of the evolution of the health crisis. On the other hand, this agreement seeks to continue the commitment to comprehensive protection of workers affected by the pandemic, both in their working environment and in their lives. Our will is and will always be to leave no one behind, and that objective obliges a constant adaptation of the measures designed, of which this royal decree-law is a clear example. Title I of the Royal Decree-Law 30/2020 of 30 September of Social Measures in Defense of Employment, faithfully reflects the agreement reached within the tripartite commission, the Social Dialogue Bureau that has led, through dialogue and negotiation, the solutions that our country has been in need of over these hard months for our economy and also for citizens. The agreement, first of all, decides on the automatic extension of all the ERTEs that were authorized when the state of alarm was declared, I repeat, of all. In this way, companies will continue to have at their disposal a measure which, although authorized for circumstances other than the current ones, continues to demonstrate its validity. In addition, in order to respond to the new limitations and containment measures necessary in the face of the outbreak of the health crisis in Spain and around the world, Royal Decree-Law 30/2020 has created two new modalities of ERTE: those called ERTE by impediment, which may request those companies whose workplaces, by new measures and decisions taken by Spanish or foreign authorities, cannot develop their activity for the time that such impossibility is maintained, and those called ERTE by limitations, which will be authorized to those companies whose activity is limited in one of their workplaces as a result of the measures that have been implemented by Spanish authorities. Both files are based decisively on very important exemptions in social security contributions. For those companies whose activity is suspended by the health decision - what we have called an impediment ERT - companies with less than 50 workers will be exempt in the next four months and as long as this situation remains 100% of social security contributions. In companies with 50 or more employees, the exemption shall be 90 %. For all those companies that have an activity imitated by health decisions - what we have called restriction ERT - protection is certainly very high. The exemption for companies with less than 50 employees will be 100% in October, 90% in November, 85% in December and 80% in January. In companies with 50 or more workers, the exemption shall be between 70 and 90 % during this period. The text reproduces the scheme of decreasing but high coefficients, which have been maintained during the previous months and which has worked in a very appropriate way in generating incentives for employment activation. The idea is clear: we must continue to provide our productive fabric, after months of shared efforts, with the protection it needs at the present time. And I do not mind reiterating this once again, public investment in the ERTE mechanism is in itself a firm commitment to permanence, a collective effort, a task for all and for all and we will not let them fall. The guarantees and limits which have so far accompanied the ERTs, the limits related to dividend sharing and tax transparency, as well as the commitment to safeguard employment for six months, shall apply to these new dossiers. In the case of undertakings which had assumed the commitment of six months fixed, as you know, by Royal Decree 8/2020, the calculation of the new safeguard period will start once it is completed. Alongside this general regime, the royal decree-law takes care of the situation of the sectors most hit by the crisis. The first additional provision sets out a number of exemptions for those companies belonging to sectors with a high rate of coverage per ERTE and, at the same time, a low rate of recovery of activity. The agreement reached in the tripartite committee objectively established the companies to which we should benefit, considering that they should include those that belong to sectors or activities that, from the beginning of the health crisis to date, have not been able to recover regulated workers in significant percentages and which also belong to sectors that have not overall recovered a normal activity. In addition, companies which certify to the competent labour authority that they belong to the value chain or indirectly depend on the activity carried out by the CNAEs listed in the annex to this Royal Decree will also have access to the recognised exemptions. In this case, companies with fewer than 50 employees will have a constant exemption of 85% of the quotas over the next 4 months and 75% for larger companies. This is a relationship that will extend to both inactive workers and those who have already been activated at this time. In the event that there is a transition from an ERTE by force majeure to an ERTE by economic reasons, the rule allows these percentages to be maintained. In line with this enhanced protection, this Royal Decree-Law establishes a new six-month employment safeguard period for companies accessing these categories, which will add, as for applicants for the new ERTE, to the six-month period already committed under the ERTE that is extended. With regard to all extended and new ERTEs, whether linked or not to exemptions in social security contributions, this text maintains the interruption of the calculation of the maximum duration of temporary contracts, as well as the prohibition of dismissal and the performance of overtime, as well as the limitations on the establishment of new outsourcings of the activity and new direct or indirect contracts. In the chapter on the protection of workers, as in the business section, this royal decree-law is the most ambitious of those adopted so far. The so-called zero counter is maintained, linked to the protection of the most vulnerable and affected by the crisis. This extraordinary protection measure will continue to apply to all persons who are dismissed or whose temporary contract is extinguished during the year 2021. Re-guarantee of the previous deficiency exemption is guaranteed, so that all persons affected by ERTE will receive unemployment benefit even if they do not have the previous contributions that would allow them to do so. The 70 % percentage on the regulatory basis is guaranteed for the purpose of calculating the amount of the benefit, after the 180 days of the benefit, with an urgent and short-term repeal of one of the most harmful sections of the labour reform promoted in 2012 by the People's Party. All workers will retain the income they were receiving, which would have been reduced if this measure had not been adopted, which, as you know, would be 50% of their salary. In addition, an extraordinary new benefit is created for persons with fixed contracts who are discontinuous or who also perform fixed and periodic work for the first time, which are repeated on certain dates, and will be extended until 31 January 2021. The text provides for financial compensation to persons receiving the special unemployment benefit in cases where their amount has been reduced in proportion to the time worked, by one or more part-time employment relationships. Finally, and in line with what the ERTE of the future will be, in response to the development needs of the working people and the increasingly demanding reality of a globalised and digitised market, a first step is being taken in linking our country's labour relations with training that must accompany people during their working and professional lives. In this case, the third additional provision of this Royal Decree sets out measures aimed at the training of persons affected by ERTE, which will be considered a priority group for access to vocational training initiatives for employment and for which specific actions will be planned. Likewise, in the context of our dialogue with self-governing associations and the good understanding that has taken place to address the social and economic consequences of this pandemic, we have agreed to re-adjust and extend specific aspects of their delivery modalities. In Title II of this Royal Decree-Law, extending also until January, the existing benefit is maintained for those self-employed who maintain a turnover of less than 75 % in this fourth quarter. The aim is to prioritise those with lower incomes and thus net returns below 1.75 times the interprofessional minimum wage. Secondly, the seasonal workers benefit is also extended, better adjusting its design to better cover the periods of earnings calculation in the reference period. Finally, two protection needs arising from the dialogue with self-government associations have been met. On the one hand, those self-employed persons who have ceased to work during the period from October to January but who have not fulfilled the above conditions, in particular as regards the minimum inter-branch wage for the ordinary benefit, will also be eligible for a benefit and, in this case, will be 50 % of their regulatory base. On the other hand, those self-employed who, either for having short periods of calculation or for having what is known as a flat rate did not enter the eligible self-employed, will also opt for a similar benefit. We therefore put in place agreed and effective instruments to allow for a transition that will allow for the gradual recovery of entrepreneurship and employment. I should also like to outline some of the elements that complement this rule. Royal Decree-Law 30/2020 incorporates a new battery of measures to improve minimum living income, in this case aimed at enabling new groups to benefit from this benefit. [[The lady]] First, persons over the age of sixty-five who do not receive any benefit, either contributory or non-contributory, are included among the potential beneficiaries of the minimum living income. Secondly, the definition of a single parent family is extended so that more of them can receive the supplement of 22 % to which they are entitled. Thirdly, it makes it easier for people who share their homes with others to apply for a minimum living income because, for example, they have rented a room; a very common remedy that many of the families at risk of exclusion have to resort to. This Royal Decree-Law also provides for the extension until 31 January of the extraordinary measures to protect the rental of housing for tenants in vulnerable situations, an expression of this Government's commitment to the social shield designed during the pandemic. It includes the extension of the period of extraordinary suspension of the eviction procedure in cases of vulnerability of tenants without housing alternatives, favouring the action of social services. In addition, the possibility of applying for a moratorium or partial cancellation of the rental rent is extended where the lessor is a large holder or public entity. It also extends the possibility of extending housing rentals for six months until 31 January 2020 under the same terms and conditions as the current contract. I conclude, not without reminding you of the extraordinary and urgent need required by article 86.1 of the Constitution for the approval by the Government of this royal decree and its additional and final provisions. The circumstances behind this legislation require, from a cyclical and structural point of view, a rapid, urgent and coordinated response within the framework of the social dialogue, as we have done. I'm done. Consolidating the ERTE, as we do with this royal decree-law for which I request its validation today, is to challenge the answer given to the same questions in previous economic crises, and it is to do so for the first time by applying European policies with internal flexibility - emphasis, internal -, with adaptation; a model embodied in our present that expressly ignores the labour legislation of the People's Party and the shortcomings of its social protection for unemployment. We're sorting out that legislation. We're avoiding mass layoffs. We are recovering the horizon of occupation that had denied us with all the tools within our reach, reformulating, as we will do, active employment policies to ensure decent and stable work, especially for women, but especially for the younger people; prioritizing training pathways, as has not been done so far, for people in an ERTE; maintaining 70% of the regulatory base, the accountant at zero, in benefits and the commitment to maintaining employment. Because we are convinced that temporality and precariousness are an anomalous and intolerable response that has prevented us from facing today's problems with more solidity and strength. Prorogation of ERTE is to reject a model of inequality that in this unexpected economic contraction caused by the pandemic has revealed its weak and imperfect seams. To extend the ERTE in a context of dialogue and agreement like the one we have reached is to look around us, stop listening - I invite you to do so - and understand that inequality, precariousness and temporality cannot fit into our labour relations. We are nothing if we are not equal and that reality can never disappear from our perception. As the French philosopher Didier Eribon points out in relation to the time it has taken for us to live, what is at stake are the most concrete realities of individual existences; among them, for example, health and well-being, ladies and gentlemen. There will be no reactivation of the economy and employment without health protection. Whoever forgets this forgets the fundamental teaching of a pandemic that has reminded us of the vital importance of public health and the social shield enabled through measures such as minimum vital income, reinforced by this very royal decree-law. The ERTE model that this legislative text enshrines and expands has saved jobs, saved rights and saved businesses. This Government and this Ministry will, as long as necessary, continue along this path, on the road to the protection of health, the economy and employment; a path that has allowed 80% of those affected by an ERTE, more than two million workers, to have already joined their jobs, recovering each and every one of their rights. We have a Europe that has also placed the protection of people at the centre of its project; we have funding and the backing of international agencies; we have a Plan for Recovery, Transformation and Resilience that will allow us to face as a country the challenges of modernizing the Spanish economy in its areas of digitisation, sustainability, innovation and ecological transition; we have a ceiling of expenditure that will strengthen investment, give solidity to the public and allow for broad and ambitious general budgets, in line with our recovery project, and we have, above all, the firm will not to let down neither the workers nor the companies. The validation of this royal decree-law will bring security and certainty to families, working people and businesses in the transit of these months so decisive for the future of the economy and employment. That is why I ask you to vote in favour of this validation and that we always continue, for the sake of dialogue, on the path of recovery, of the reconstruction of our country. Thank you very much. REAL DECRETO-LEY 31/2020 OF 29 SEPTEMBER, adopting urgent measures in the field of non-universal education. [[File number 130/000032]]
ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u156	We continue with the agenda: Validation or repeal of Royal Decree-Law 31/2020 of 29 September, by which urgent measures are taken in the field of non-university education. To present the Royal Decree-Law, the Minister of Education and Vocational Training has the floor on behalf of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u157	Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen. I am writing to you to request the validation of Royal Decree-Law 31/2020 of 29 September, by which urgent measures are taken in the field of non-university education. This is a rule that contains a set of measures that have been discussed and discussed in depth with the Autonomous Communities, such as competent administrations in the field of education, during several meetings of the various bodies of the Sectoral Conference on Education, and more specifically that held on 24 September 2020. Measures that undoubtedly come to respond to the concerns that were expressed there. The pandemic has had a special impact on the education system, which, despite the difficulties that have arisen, has adapted to change quickly, decisively and effectively. The Ministry of Education and Vocational Training and the autonomous administrations have supported this effort by the educational community through the adoption of the necessary agreements to enable the continuity of the teaching activity, the adjustment of the programming and the adaptation of the evaluation, promotion and graduation mechanisms. All these measures became binding agreements for the Autonomous Communities, which they adopted at the conferences held on 15 April and 11 June, without at any time changing their competences in the field of education. As a follow-up to this collaborative work, this royal decree supports several issues that enable a better development of the 2020-2021 course in the current pandemic situation. As I said before, the Government thus responds to a number of concerns that the Autonomous Communities have been exposing at successive meetings that have taken place since May to organize the 2020-2021 school year. Education and the safe functioning of educational institutions have become priority social concerns which also deserve priority attention from the public authorities. We must respond to this situation with rigour, measure, responsibility and commitment, for which it is necessary to launch new exceptional and temporary initiatives. To this end, the Royal Decree-Law establishes four blocks of measures that respond to the extraordinary circumstances resulting from the pandemic and will allow the course to develop in a safe and adequate manner, ensuring appropriate attention to the entire student. The first block of measures is aimed at non-university teaching staff with the aim of ensuring adequate and safe development of teaching. In the current situation, the protocols designed by the Government and the educational administrations for the safe return to academic activity force the reinforcement of the staff in an urgent manner; this may in some cases - certainly few but not negligible - mean that not enough suitable candidates are available. It is not acceptable that there are groups of students who do not have teachers, so the Royal Decree allows, on an exceptional and temporary basis and with caution to ensure that students have the best teachers, the recruitment of teachers who meet all the required requirements even if they have not yet completed the specific master's degree. The objective is to ensure the coverage of those places that are necessary to provide adequate care to all students, places that in this specific case will be depreciated at the end of the course in which the reasons for urgent need that gave rise to their creation disappear. Thus, only in the event of the exhaustion of the lists of candidates for temporary employment and, in addition, of applicants for employment who are in possession of the official post-graduate degree attesting to their pedagogical and didactic or equivalent training, educational administrations may, on an exceptional basis, appoint as interim officials those applicants for the requested specialties who do not at that time meet the requirement to be in possession of the official post-graduate or equivalent educational and didactic qualification. We comply with article 100.2 and the ninth additional provision of the Organic Law 2/2006 of 3 May on Education, Royal Decree 1834/2008 of 8 November and Order EDU/2645/2011 of 23 September. In this way, and as repeatedly requested by the Autonomous Communities, they will be able to carry out the reinforcement of the necessary staffing to guarantee the right to education in safety during the course 2020-2021. This provision includes the bodies of secondary school teachers, vocational training technicians, music teachers, performing arts, visual arts and design and official language schools. The Royal Decree also provides that the continuity in the teaching of these aspirants, as well as their eventual subsequent entry into the Public Service, will require that they be in possession of the postgraduate master's degree. The measures in the field of teaching also include the commitment to the continuity of the processes of decreasing the rate of temporary employment in the teaching field, through the call of the places offered within the plan of stabilization of temporary employment, objective that was supported by the educational administrations at the meeting of the Sectoral Conference of Education held on April 15, 2020. To this end, it is ensured that the provisions of the third transitional provision of the Regulation on admission, access and acquisition of new specialties in the teaching bodies referred to in the Organic Law 2/2006 of 3 May of Education, approved by Royal Decree 276/2007 of 23 February, also apply to the selective procedures of admission carried out in execution of the public offers of employment which, under the provisions of the Law 6/2018 of the General Budgets of the State for the year 2018, are approved by the different educational administrations until 2021 to ensure the uniformity and consistency of this stabilization process aimed at the decided decrease of the rates of interinity. The second block introduces measures for the adaptation of curricula and curricula, as well as the adequacy of evaluation, promotion and qualification criteria. The aim of all of them is nothing other than to respond to the different situations generated by the pandemic during the past and current years, allowing students to continue their learning process with the greatest guarantees, giving educational administrations the opportunity to plan their actions in sufficient time, providing security to the different components of the educational community and guaranteeing the quality of academic degrees. Therefore, in order to facilitate the adaptation of didactic programming to the circumstances arising from the past and current courses, it is given the character of guidelines to the evaluable learning standards. In this way, the autonomous communities and the centres themselves will be able to adapt their teaching programmes to the actual situation of their pupils and to the forms of educational activity resulting from the pandemic. Similarly, educational administrations are allowed to adapt the assessment and promotion criteria for all primary, compulsory secondary, baccalaureate and vocational education courses, as well as the criteria for obtaining a degree in compulsory secondary education, baccalaureate and vocational training qualifications, in order to better adapt them to the situation caused by the pandemic. The teaching teams, based on their professional competence, in the exercise of their professionalism, will assess in a collegial, non-isolated way - and this is what is relevant, ladies and gentlemen, for their added value in terms of more guarantee and better criterion - and overall if the objectives of each stage have been achieved and they will make the decisions corresponding to them in that process. Who better than those teaching teams to know the academic evolution of the student and whether he deserves to progress or not? Thus, the titling decision will be taken after ensuring the acquisition of the general objectives of the stage, so that the student can continue his or her academic itinerary. It will therefore be held after acquiring the overall objectives of the stage. Therefore, the claim that it can be held with suspense is simply false. [[Applauds]] In adopting these measures, the majority agreements of the Sectoral Conference of 15 April 2020, which twelve autonomous communities signed and which all, ladies and gentlemen, ended up implementing - with good results, by the way - beyond their initial pronouncement on this matter, are being implemented. It is not, therefore, a novel measure, but a continuation of what was agreed and implemented in the 2019-2020 course that we succeeded in substantiating. So, ladies and gentlemen, what we are pushing is more effort, more effort and not less, more effort of our students and more effort of the whole educational system. The evaluations of the final stage of Primary Education and Compulsory Secondary Education, on the other hand, that from the Royal Decree-Law 5/2016, of 9 December, have lost all academic effect, having exclusively a diagnostic purpose, are suppressed. With regard to the school calendar, school days shall be considered all those on which the student is given care and educational support, either in person or at a distance. The measures contained in this Royal Decree also extend to artistic, sports and official language schools; specifically for the latter, it is envisaged that the criteria for evaluation, promotion and permanence will be changed, as well as that the common basic principles for evaluation applicable to the official certification tests of intermediate levels B1, intermediate levels B2, advanced levels C1 and advanced levels C2 of special regime language teachings will be brought into line with the exceptional circumstances arising from the pandemic. The third block specifies that even non-university private centres, which may encounter difficulties similar to those of public centres when completing their staff, can do so, exceptionally and only during the duration of the pandemic caused by COVID-19, with persons who meet all the requirements, except for pedagogical and didactic training provided for in Article 100.2 of Organic Law 2/2006 of 3 May, Education or equivalent. Finally, the fourth block sets out measures to organize and organize education in the field of vocational training in the education system, which will apply in cases where its development is impossible in accordance with the basic rules of the State. Having recognized and underlined the importance of practice in student enterprises and its irreplaceable nature, the current circumstances could make the development of such practices in certain sectors and in certain territories unworkable. It is therefore appropriate to allow from the basic regulations a flexibility that only in those essential cases allows students to complete their studies. In this way, the exceptional reduction of the training module in workplaces may be authorised, in any case respecting the minimum hours laid down in the Royal Decrees establishing each title and its minimum teaching. In addition, the competent educational authorities may, where there is no viable alternative, replace the stay in their own enterprises of training in workplaces with a proposal for activities associated with the working environment. In the case of higher vocational training, this training module in work centres can be integrated into the project module, and in the case of basic and intermediate vocational training, a project module can be created to integrate it with training in work centres. It will be possible, ladies and gentlemen. This measure is also extended to artistic and sports educations, with appropriate adaptations to their respective characteristics. Apart from the teaching of the education system and with regard to the certificates of professionalism of the Vocational Training for Employment, regulated by Royal Decree 34/2008 of 18 January, exceptional measures are also laid down during the 2020-2021 school year for the realization of the module of practical training in workplaces where it is not possible to carry out effectively in a business area with a similar criterion to that used in the vocational training of the education system. Ladies and gentlemen, I believe that the principle of necessity has been fully justified. Unesco has been warning us for months about the dramatic effects of the pandemic on education. Teacher, management teams, families, students, all have been able to adapt quickly to a radical change in the way of education. We have met the goal of reopening schools, but for the duration of the health emergency we must ensure a consistent normative basis for the course to continue to develop in the best way, guaranteeing learning and, above all, preventing someone from staying behind. For all that, ladies and gentlemen, I ask for your support to validate this rule, as it is essential to support the measures required for the proper development of the school year, with adequate and proportionate answers to what the health situation is demanding; because it facilitates the actions that the autonomous communities are necessarily having to carry out to complete their teaching staff; because it establishes academic measures to enable learning to be carried out effectively, and because it contributes to the coordination of all educational administrations. As you can see, ladies and gentlemen, this is a set of necessary measures, measured and elaborated with the participation and contributions of the Autonomous Communities, which therefore have a high degree of consensus. I hope that today we will be able to reach a broad agreement on this royal decreeley so that the educational administrations and the whole educational community can have the necessary tools to be able to continue working on the functioning of the Spanish educational system. Ladies and gentlemen, I trust that this can be the case and we can continue to work towards improving our education. Thank you very much. Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applauds]]

Does it respond to a specific and specific need in an emergency situation? Yeah. That is why we are voting in favour. ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u183	Thank you very much, Mr. Legarda. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Thank you so much. For the Citizens' Parliamentary Group, Mrs Giménez Giménez has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u184	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, today is a day that changes Spain for the better. From Citizens we proposed an initiative that in the midst of the pain of the pandemic, of concern, brings us a ray of hope. Ladies and gentlemen, the reform of the Criminal Code in so far as it does not allow for forced sterilization, we believe that it is a great step forward on equal terms. I am sure that many people may find it unthinkable that forced sterilizations are carried out in the 21st century in our country, it seems to be a matter of the past, of the dark part of our past that we have left behind, but it is not. We propose this reform because our article 156.2 allows for the forced sterilization of persons with disabilities or incapacitated judicially, an exception that affects women with disabilities, who are prevented from being mothers by force; and not by any force, ladies and gentlemen, by force of law. In an advanced democracy like ours, it is surprising that the right of persons with disabilities to form a family without any discrimination has been ignored. Forced or non-consensual sterilization in cases where it is still permitted is an anomaly in our human rights protection system. It will respect the International Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities of 2006, ratified by our State in 2008; the Istanbul Convention; the protection of personal integrity and, from a gender perspective, the Convention on the Elimination of All Forms of Discrimination against Women. We are therefore surprised that this anomaly has been allowed in our system until now, because it has no justification. Therefore, from Citizens we were clear that we could not spend a single minute more without adjusting our legal framework to the European framework, to the international framework and to the recommendations of the international bodies in this field, namely that of the Committee on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities in 2016 and again in 2019. I firmly believe in the equality of all people, and I am therefore proud to have promoted an amendment to our Criminal Code in order to eliminate forever from our legislation that exception that allows for the forced sterilization of persons with disabilities, an initiative that has been defending Cermi, and specifically Cermi Women, for years to come, to whom I thank for their work. Furthermore, we are proud to have managed to bring this reform forward with broad consensus, something that we should be much more accustomed to in this House; a consensus to which we parliamentary groups must come, giving political size. Why? For issues of equality, for issues of inclusion, for issues that improve our society, especially at this time. In these moments of COVID is the spirit that from Citizens we want to promote, build a better society and reach agreements. That's useful politics. In addition, we are faced with a reform that will have a translation in the lives of many women, of disabled women who will have their guaranteed right to be mothers. On the occasion of my parliamentary work, I have heard various testimonies from women who have been stripped of something as basic and as profound as the ability to create life. As a mother, it upsets my stomach; as a person, it outrages me. Because these women are taken away from being mothers because of their disability, as if only because of that condition could they be denied the right to form a family. Moreover, the worst thing about hearing these testimonies is that we see that there are women who believe in it. Disappointed, resigned, they accept that they do not deserve to be mothers. Therefore, I believe it is a reform that will improve the physical, emotional and motivational health of so many women. Furthermore, we are aware that this is a first stone on this path because there is a great deal of work to be done for the equality and inclusion of persons with disabilities, and our parliamentary group will be on that path. ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u186	Thank you, Madam President. Various international agencies and institutions - including the UN - have recognized that forced sterilization of persons with disabilities can be considered a form of torture and constitutes a violation of human rights. Practices such as this are based on prejudices arising from paternalism and the childishization of persons with disabilities, which are not recognized as sufficiently discerning to make decisions about their own sexuality and reproduction, thus denying the dignity and integrity of persons with disabilities. Despite the progress made, the legislation of many countries, including the Spanish State, to date continues to allow judges, health professionals, family members or guardians to validly supplement the will and consent of the disabled person to be sterilized or sterilized for his or her best interests. We are talking about disabled people, but it should be remembered that the vast majority of them are girls and young women, adding a number of factors that aggravate discrimination, such as youth, disability and gender, and that Spanish law has just recognized the ability of women in general to decide on their own bodies. Sterilization in the name of this alleged best interest does not protect them from sexual violence or from abuse and aggression. This legislative reform is therefore necessary and urgent. We will thus prevent girls and young people with disabilities from being subjected without their consent to sterilization procedures and to do so under the general assumption of their inability to properly manage decisions about their sexual life, reproduction and possible motherhood. For these, as for the whole population in general, the key, in addition to this legislative reform, is undoubtedly full and taboo sex education. In addition, there is a need to educate and sensitize society at large on the sexual and reproductive health and rights of women with disabilities and to eliminate all forms of violence against them. That is why we are voting in favour. Thank you.

ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u189	Thank you very much, Mr. Miquel. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Mrs Saavedra Muñoz has the floor for the Parliamentary Group of the United Nations Podemos-En Comú Podem-Galicia in Communal.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-10-15-CD201015.u190	Good night. Thank you, President. This bill, which we hope to adopt today, is a great achievement in terms of the rights of all persons with disabilities, but especially for women. Today, with the repeal of article 156 of the Criminal Code, the possibility of forced sterilization, a serious violation of fundamental human rights that still existed in our legal system, is eliminated. This measure has been pending since the Spanish State ratified the International Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, ratified in 2008. It is a further step in the abandonment of that paternalistic framework which considers persons with disabilities only as the object of protection and care, in order to move towards a plan of equality, of guaranteeing fundamental rights and the recognition that all people, each with our functional diversity, must have the capacity to decide on our own life. The UN Special Rapporteur on the rights of persons with disabilities stated in 2017 that women with disabilities have many problems in deciding for themselves about their sexual and reproductive health, and are systematically exposed to violence, abuse and harmful practices such as sterilization, abortion or forced contraception. In February 2018, Cermi Mujeres and the European Disability Forum, together with other entities, demanded to end this unconsensual practice in women and girls in our country. The United Nations Committee on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, in 2019, reiterated its concern that women continue to be subjected to sterilization and forced abortion and urged the Spanish State to repeal article 156. That is what we will do today, facing a problem that is not residual. According to data from the General Council of the Judiciary, in the last ten years about a thousand women have been sterilized in our country. From Unidad Podemos-En Comú Podemos-Galicia in Comú we understand that responding to the demands of this collective is a question of rights, it is a democratic demand and cannot depend on goodwill or budget availability. This coalition government and this parliamentary group with this and other initiatives are derailing the path taken by the governments of the People's Party that punished people with disabilities with their neoliberal policies of austerity. They virtually demolished the Law on Dependency and Personal Autonomy, cut subsidies and benefits, imposed co-payments, cut resources to support autonomy, educational inclusion and employment and social inclusion. This coalition government is working in a context as difficult as the current one, as the one we are living in, so that all people, regardless of their functional diversity, achieve total equality, and it was reflected in the government agreement. At present they are in various states of procedure in this House: a bill to regulate cognitive accessibility; another important reform of legislation on the legal capacity of persons with disabilities, which ends incapacitation, replacing it with a support system for free decision-making; the education law, which will lead to progress in educational inclusion; the child and adolescent protection law, which promotes early care, among other things, and in addition the coalition Government is working on a shock plan to improve the system of care for dependency and the promotion of personal autonomy, which will be reflected in State budgets. With all this we comply with the international conventions signed, but we also meet the just demands of groups of disability, functional diversity and women who, with tireless work, whom we want to thank and value from here, have been for a long time denouncing discrimination, fighting for their rights, demanding equality and proposing alternatives. We cannot ignore these demands. The rule that we will adopt today not only prevents forced sterilization, but also commits itself to strengthening the Law of 2002 on patient autonomy, and the Law of 2010 on sexual and reproductive health, with two objectives. First, that people who, as patients, have to make decisions and need support have clinical information and documentation in accessible formats, channels and supports so that their decision is free and informed. Secondly, the obligation of the public authorities to guarantee the right to sexual and reproductive health of persons with disabilities is reinforced. We believe that it is essential to make progress on this guarantee of access to information and support, and on the training of sexual and reproductive health. This is essential for the entire population, but especially for people with functional diversity, to exercise their rights and avoid abuse. We know that the right often regards sexual and reproductive health training as indoctrination if it does not coincide with its ideology, but international organizations demand it, consider it essential, and we will make progress in this regard. This coalition government and our parliamentary group have a strong commitment to end discrimination of all kinds, to make progress in promoting the autonomy of everyone in affective-sexual education, to avoid abuse, to support freely assumed motherhood and to alleviate difficult socio-economic situations that make life and future projects impossible for people. Today, ladies and gentlemen, it is an important day, today we continue to build a more dignified, fair and inclusive country. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

[[Applauds]] Today we are discussing the proposal for a law amending the Criminal Code for the eradication of forced or non-consensual sterilization of persons with disabilities who are legally disabled. The Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, in its article 23, on respect for the home and the family, paragraph 1, provides that States parties shall take effective and relevant measures to put an end to discrimination against persons with disabilities in all matters relating to marriage, family, paternity, and urge member countries to ensure that their fertility is maintained on an equal basis with others. That treaty was ratified by Spain in 2007 and entered into force in 2008, a ratification which, as you know, ladies and gentlemen, implies an acceptance of its legal obligations under the treaty and which therefore obliges us to adapt our legislation and to establish public policies to enforce the obligations acquired. The Council of Europe Convention on the Prevention and Combating of Violence against Women, known as the Istanbul Convention, ratified by Spain in March 2014, makes it a criminal act in article 39 to criminalize surgical interventions that will end a woman's ability to reproduce naturally without her prior and informed consent. In addition, the Committee on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, the United Nations body responsible for ensuring compliance with the Convention, in its last report, drafted from a human rights perspective, with regard to the improvements to be faced by Spain, the first is the elimination of forced sterilization. Today we are here to legislate in favour of this measure, with the amendment of article 156 of the Criminal Code in order to eradicate the forced sterilization of persons with disabilities from our legal system, and we do so not only as an obligation to comply with our international commitments, but, more importantly, we do so on the understanding that with this legislative step we take a great step as a society, a great step towards guaranteeing equality and life in dignity for persons with disabilities. Forced sterilization affects men and women, although to a much greater extent women and girls, and it is therefore normal for us to focus on them, given that they suffer double discrimination: because of their disability and their status as women. The testimonies of those who have suffered it are shocking because this practice, ladies and gentlemen, involves a denial of their status as a person and an attack on their bodily integrity which, in cases of psychosocial disability, traumatizes the victim and can have consequences on their mental health for life and, which is extremely serious, can increase vulnerability to sexual abuse. Well, today we are discussing a text that welcomes Cermi, which - let us not forget - is the independent body responsible for monitoring the implementation of the convention here in Spain, a text that comes, after its processing in the Senate, with a broad consensus. The issues related to disability dealt with here in Congress have also always enjoyed a great consensus, such as - I remind you - the one achieved in the XII Legislature with a Government of the People's Party, in which the amendment of the Electoral Law recognized the right of more than 100,000 people with intellectual disabilities to vote as full citizens, and was adopted unanimously. For the sake of that consensus, the People's Party, in its proceedings here in the Lower House, did not make any amendments to the original text. However, the Socialist Party introduced in the paper two amendments that we understood from the very beginning that they had no other purpose than to break that consensus, some amendments aimed at amending two laws: the Basic Law regulating patient autonomy and the Law on Sexual and Reproductive Health, without specifying which articles and in what terms such amendments will be made, amendments - I repeat - aimed at breaking the consensus that we understand was guaranteed from the very beginning with just maintaining the initial text. The efforts of the PSOE have not only not been in the sense of seeking such consensus, but quite the contrary: we understand that efforts have been made to break it.
